12     Continuous-pair Bedford–Taylor independence
Active items: P21.3. Status: Open in the residual scope.

12.1    Atomic targets
P21.3 (T2): Bedford–Taylor independence for continuous equal-curvature pairs
For a complete non-Archimedean ﬁeld and a good separated strict boundaryless pure n-
dimensional analytic space, n ≥ 2, decide whether every positive d0 - and d00 -closed CLD
(p, p)-current, 0 ≤ p ≤ n − 1, has Bedford–Taylor independence on every ordinary open for
continuous psh pairs with equal Hessian currents. The full quantiﬁed equations appear below.
A negative answer must give an analytic counterexample in this scope, not just a defective
abstract tropical pushforward.

12.2    What constitutes a solution
Prove the quantiﬁed every-open, every-current continuous-pair assertion, or supply an analytic
counterexample with all ambient hypotheses. A ﬁxed polycylinder, a cover of one speciﬁed
open and a genuine ordinary-open basis give diﬀerent conclusions. Weak approximability is a
suﬃcient condition, not a density theorem for arbitrary currents.

12.3    Full statement and detailed state of the art
The two original realization questions have been settled and retired. The full unbounded cross
cannot literally be the image of a harmonic tropicalization whose source is compact, by Book
Proposition 69.2.3. Conversely, Book Proposition 69.2.4 constructs a reduced boundaryless
analytic curve with a proper noncompact tropicalization onto the cross, exhausted by compact
harmonic-tropicalization charts, and an eﬀective cycle whose current has weights (2, 2, 1, 1).
That construction realizes the psh approximants and the defect after proper pushforward, but
the two curvature currents are unequal before pushforward. It therefore does not answer the
analytic Bedford–Taylor question upstairs.
     The normal-curve theorem Book Proposition 16.4.8 settles the ordinary current problem
on every connected good separated normal boundaryless pure analytic curve over a complete
non-Archimedean ﬁeld, possibly trivially valued: every positive closed current of bidegree
(0, 0) is cδY . It requires neither strictness nor a smoothness hypothesis and gives the full CLD
Bedford–Taylor predicate.
     The componentwise theorem Book Proposition 16.4.9 settles the displayed continuous-pair
problem for every good separated strict boundaryless pure one-dimensional analytic space
over either valuation regime, without reducedness or a smoothness hypothesis on component
normalizations. If (Xi )i are the irreducible components of Xred , every positive closed current
of bidegree (0, 0) has the unique locally ﬁnite expression

    The endpoint base now also supports proper and mixed ﬁbres. In Book Theorem M.1.25,
each stage may be a locally trivial standard strict open-polydisc bundle, a projective-space
bundle, a smooth proper split-toric bundle, or—over an algebraically closed ﬁeld—a proper

on the entire top-level space is the pullback of a unique logarithmic-aﬃne function on the
endpoint polycylinder, hence is ordinary CLD-smooth with zero smooth Hessian. The whole-

Corollary M.1.62 therefore give Bedford–Taylor independence for arbitrary positive closed
ordinary CLD currents in the corresponding scopes. The cover proof ﬁrst globalizes the smooth
zero-Hessian diﬀerence and does not invoke current sheaf gluing outside its stated hypotheses.
Projective, split-toric, and proper-curve stages retain the exact local holomorphic-separation
and paracompactness requirements of their descent theorems.
    This narrows the higher-dimensional residual; it does not turn a polycylinder cover into a
basis and does not settle arbitrary analytic germs.
    There is now a second, genuinely ﬁbred basis range controlled by the residual geometry of
a curve rather than by an explicit polycylinder formula. For a smooth proper curve over an

    The annular stages may now also occur at arbitrary levels among the four exact bun-

Proposition M.1.35 shows that an analytically locally trivial ﬁnite strict open-polyannulus bun-

action on every tower level, and equivariance of every bundle map; it neither treats pairs
with noninvariant diﬀerence nor descends arbitrary currents through an algebraic-splitting
extension. This conclusion is local in the spatial variable. For one speciﬁed open set, an

weighted-gluing defect before tropical pushforward.
     No one-dimensional case remains inside the displayed ambient hypotheses. Indeed, Book
Proposition 16.4.9 treats either valuation, arbitrary reducibility and nilpotent structure, and
normalizations with no smoothness assumption. Non-good, non-separated, non-strict, and
boundary cases lie outside the displayed problem rather than forming hidden residual atoms.
Within dimension at least two, spaces with the strict-open polycylinder basis of Book Corol-
lary 16.4.2 are also removed from the residual scope, as are ﬁxed-domain pairs on one standard
strict open polycylinder. The invariant split-toric family, including its toric boundary strata,
of Book Proposition 16.13.1 is likewise removed. The atom remains open for general spaces
in the displayed ambient class.
     The problem uses the book’s continuous-potential convention. Literal [CLD25, Deﬁni-
tion 8.6.2] quantiﬁes over the full CLD psh class, which may include discontinuous func-
tions. The normal-curve theorem establishes that stronger statement, while the reducible
and nonreduced componentwise theorem establishes the continuous-pair predicate used here.
The remaining discontinuous-potential question in the latter setting is neither asserted to be
equivalent to the displayed problem nor separately counted here. Thus P21.3 remains one
genuinely open residual T2 atom, now only in analytic dimension at least two and outside
the veriﬁed basis, ﬁxed-domain, and invariant split-toric regimes above. The book-wide inven-
tory remains 12 open problem environments and 42 active atomic questions, with tier totals
0/3/7/15/17 for T1 through T5.
     The four-punctured smooth-curve obstruction remains useful as a warning about the
method. Its coarse tropical weights (2, 2, 1, 1) violate reﬁned balancing after a third ana-
lytic unit is appended, so a coarse tropical pushforward cannot replace analytic classiﬁcation.
The well-deﬁned residual atom is precisely the continuous-pair CLD question in the ranges not
covered by Book Proposition 16.4.9, Book Corollary 16.4.2, Book Proposition 16.4.15, Book
Proposition 16.13.1. Equality only after a noninjective tropical pushforward does not decide
it.
     There is an exact suﬃcient route for the remaining analytic question. Put h = U − V .
If, locally on W , the continuous zero-Hessian function h is a locally uniform limit of smooth

functions hj satisfying d0 d00 hj = 0, then Book Corollary 16.13.1 gives d0 d00 (hT ) = 0, hence the
required equality for every positive closed T . Thus the unresolved continuous-pair problem
is concentrated at higher-dimensional gluing loci where the displayed smooth pluriharmonic
approximation is not known. Merely knowing d0 d00 [h] = 0 does not supply the approximants
(Book Corollary 16.13.1); the abstract cross-fan shows why cellwise aﬃne data alone cannot
replace them. Nor do the bare real-PL or GJR-harmonic conclusions occurring in P06.1/P06.2:
that G-local, weakly smooth regularity does not provide ordinary CLD-smooth zero-Hessian
approximants. Indeed, Book Proposition 19.3.2 gives an integral PL weakly smooth harmonic
function on a curve with no locally uniform ordinary CLD-smooth harmonic approximation.
This obstructs this approximation route, not the separately proved one-dimensional P21.3
classiﬁcation. This bridge is related to, but is not identiﬁed with, P06.2. At the level of
strong currents there is nevertheless a formal compatibility: Book Proposition 18.9.2 gives
d0 d00 (h ∧ S) = 0 for a weakly smooth zero-Hessian h and a closed strong current S. This is
deliberately not used to settle P21.3. The latter uses the order-zero ordinary product hT ; even
when an ordinary current T has a strong lift, its equality with the restriction of h ∧ S requires
a separate compatibility argument, as spelled out in Book Section 18.9. There is now one
genuine ordinary-current bridge inside this distinction. If T is locally weak-form-induced in the
sense of Book Deﬁnition 18.9.2, and the diﬀerence h = U − V is a weakly smooth zero-Hessian
function, then Book Proposition 18.9.3 proves the required equality. Its proof identiﬁes the
order-zero product hT with the restriction of the strong product by uniform ordinary-smooth
approximation on compact supports and the C 0 continuity of weakly smooth integration. It
covers neither an arbitrary strong lift nor an arbitrary positive closed current, and it still
assumes weak smoothness of h; it is therefore a current-and-pair subcase, not a resolution of
the remaining gluing problem. The bridge now persists through two natural but explicitly
conditional closures. If the ordinary current is locally a weak limit of positive closed locally
weak-form-induced currents, and h is locally a uniform limit of weakly smooth zero-Hessian
functions, then Book Proposition 18.9.4 gives the same ordinary-current equality. The proof
ﬁrst passes the form-induced identity along the current net and then along the function net,
using the order-zero multiplication-continuity lemma at each step. It is not a density theorem
for arbitrary positive closed CLD currents or arbitrary continuous zero-Hessian diﬀerences, so
it narrows a paired closure class without removing the unresolved higher-dimensional gluing
step. The exact P06.2 proper-full-rank moment classiﬁcation supplies this bridge for a genuine
pairwise subcase: if h has such a factorization locally, then Book Corollary M.1.21 gives the
required equality for every positive closed current. P06.2 supplies no such local factorization
for an arbitrary h, however, so this new exclusion is a condition on a pair of potentials rather
than a resolution of the higher-dimensional gluing problem.

BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: TASK-GRANTED exact strict real-PL argument, only within its full stated scope
# Strict real-PL recognition and ordinary Bedford–Taylor multipliers

Source-proof repair and partial progress on P21.3. Research draft, 3 October 2026.
The unrestricted atom remains unresolved. No priority or novelty claim is made.

## 1. Exact result and target

The target allows an arbitrary complete non-Archimedean field K, and a good,
separated, strict, boundaryless K-analytic space X, pure of dimension n >= 2.
It quantifies over every ordinary open W, every positive d'- and d''-closed
ordinary CLD (p,p)-current T, 0 <= p <= n-1, and every finite continuous CLD
psh pair U,V on W. It asks whether

    dd[U] = dd[V]  =>  dd(UT|W) = dd(VT|W),   dd = d'd''.

Here [f]=f delta_X is the function current, with the canonical generic
component multiplicities, and fT is the ordinary order-zero product.
Positivity throughout means CLD's middle positivity: symmetry and
nonnegative pairing against positive complementary test forms. We never
replace this by tests against only strongly positive forms.

The repaired result restricts K to NONTRIVIAL valuation and the FUNCTION
class to Gubler–Jell–Rabinoff's actual R-PL class. An integral PL function
is continuous and G-locally log|u| for analytic units u, using the G-topology
generated by STRICT affinoid domains. In CLD terminology this is STRICTLY
integral parlinear (12.5.2), not merely integral parlinear with arbitrary
real constants on possibly nonstrict domains. An R-PL function is G-locally
a finite real combination of such logarithms (GJR Definition 5.1).

**Main partial theorem.** Under the target's ambient hypotheses and
nontrivial valuation, for every ordinary open W and every GJR R-PL h on W:

1. If dd[h] >= 0, then h belongs to CPsh(W), with CPsh defined solely by
   CLD Definition 8.1.7. Its residue is nef at every point.
2. Conversely, a semipositive GJR R-PL function belongs to CPsh, and a CPsh
   function has positive Hessian current. Thus on this precise R-PL class
   positive current, residual nefness, and CPsh membership are equivalent.
3. If dd[h]=0, both h and -h are CPsh, and dd(hT)=0 for EVERY target
   positive doubly closed ordinary current T. T is neither approximated
   nor lifted to a strong current.
4. The last identity persists if locally, on one fixed ordinary neighbourhood,
   h is a locally uniform limit of a NET of GJR R-PL zero-current functions.
   This asserts multiplier closure, not CPsh net closure or density.

Consequently the target identity holds for all finite continuous pairs
whose difference lies in this class or the displayed net closure; the
incoming pair need not itself be converted from Psh to CPsh.

We also give an actual strict boundaryless smooth analytic surface and a
strict PL function h which is not ordinary CLD-smooth, yet has both signs
in CPsh and satisfies the multiplier identity for all target currents.
This is a worked obstruction to a proposed counterexample, not a
counterexample to P21.3.

## 2. Primary inputs and their exact scopes

References [CLD] and [GJR] mean respectively arXiv:1204.6277v2 (25 July
2025) and arXiv:2111.05741v3 (7 March 2025). Both primary texts and PDFs
were read locally. Bibliographic theorem numbers below refer to these
versions. The book's Appendix M is an input to audit, not a granted proof.
Review receipts identify issues; none is used as a mathematical premise.

The indispensable geometric inputs are as follows.

**G1 (ordinary PL decomposition and formal models).** On a good strict
space over complete nontrivially valued K, a GJR R-PL function has at every
point an ORDINARY open neighbourhood on which it is a finite real sum of
integral PL functions (GJR Proposition 5.8). Integral PL functions on a
paracompact space are weights of formal models of the trivial bundle
(5.6); the formal models are filtered by dominance (2.4). Corollary 5.9
gives arbitrarily small compact strict neighbourhoods and one formal model
for a finite presentation. No reducedness, smoothness, algebraic closure,
or discretely valued hypothesis is present here.

For clarity, the nontrivial ordinary-local step in 5.8 works as follows.
Take a finite strict G-cover around x and group all coefficients into a
Q-independent basis, clearing its finite rational denominators. On two
overlapping domains, concatenate the logarithms of units. The germ of
their tropical image has rational tangent directions (GJR 3.2–3.3).
Rational displacement vectors are dense in each polytope of that germ.
Equality of the original real combinations and Q-independence force the
individual logarithms, after subtracting their values at x, to agree on
the overlaps. Their differences are constant there. Evaluate those
constants at rigid points: they belong to Q Gamma, Gamma=log|K^*|.
One integer multiple places all finitely many constants in Gamma;
multiply the units by constants from K^*, then glue. Dividing that integer
back into the real coefficients gives integral PL summands on one ordinary
neighbourhood. Thus this is not a promotion of a G-cover into an open cover
without the needed argument.

**G2 (residues, models, and nefness).** For a good strict germ, its ungraded
reduction red(X,x) is a quasi-compact open in the Riemann–Zariski space
of tilde H(x)/tilde K. On a formal model Y of a compact strict
neighbourhood, the closure V_Y,x of red_Y(x) is a model of this reduction;
the restriction of a formal line bundle to V_Y,x represents the residue
(GJR 6.13, 6.16; CLD Lemmas 11.9.2 and 17.7.10). Residues are additive,
and extend real-linearly to R-PL germs (GJR Lemma 6.18). They are intrinsic
to the germ. The Picard group of the reduction is the filtered colimit
of model Picard groups (GJR 6.6). A proper dominating morphism gives
another model (6.2), and models are filtered (Lemma 6.5).

A line bundle, or a real class, on a model is nef iff its degree on every
proper integral curve is nonnegative. Nefness on the reduction can be
checked on ANY representing model (GJR Lemma 6.11). To check the latter
assertion, take a common model of a nef representative and the given
representative, then a further model on which their pullbacks agree in
Pic tensor R. Proper pullback preserves nefness by the curve projection
formula. Proper surjective descent also holds: a proper integral curve
on the base is dominated by a proper curve upstairs, obtained by taking
the closure of a closed point over its generic point. The projection
formula gives the converse inequality. This proves the model-independence
needed here, without choosing a preferred model of all reductions.

**G3 (strict lifting and properness).** At an inner point of a compact
strict neighbourhood Y, V_Y,x is proper over tilde K (CLD Lemma 11.9.2(d)).
By Chow's lemma a proper integral variety V_0 admits a proper birational
morphism V -> V_0 with V projective. It is still a reduction model by
the valuative criterion (GJR 6.2). Every line bundle on red(X,x) is the
strict residue of a STRICTLY integral metrized analytic line-bundle germ
whose underlying analytic line bundle is trivial near x (CLD Theorem
17.5.7(b), or Proposition 17.5.6). A chosen analytic frame therefore
provides a genuine integral GJR PL weight a, with the prescribed residue.
Good implies almost good, as required by that lifting theorem; strictness
and separatedness are retained.

The strict lifting proof is the degree-one version of CLD Proposition
17.4.7: use a finite strict-domain cover trivializing the residual bundle,
lift the degree-one residual transition isomorphisms to analytic
isomorphisms near x, and shrink finitely often so they are isometries on
the corresponding domain intersections (strict Lemma 17.5.3 and
Corollary 17.5.5). With one domain fixed, form the other transitions by
u_beta,gamma=u_alpha,beta^(-1)u_alpha,gamma; these satisfy the cocycle
identities exactly, rather than just modulo reduction. Glue the metrics.
For rank one the underlying bundle can be trivialized by the same
transitions on the ordinary germ. Local orthonormal frames have norm 1,
so the resulting weight is G-strict-locally a logarithm of a unit with
NO arbitrary real additive constant. This explains why this lifting step
has the strict scope needed for the denominator argument.

**G4 (cofinality and semipositive locus).** Given any reduction model V,
some compact strict neighbourhood Y and formal model have V_Y,x
dominating V (GJR Lemma 6.14). If two line-bundle models give the same
residue, their pullbacks agree after a further dominating model, by the
filtered-colimit description of Pic. Formal models and inclusions of
compact neighbourhoods can be made compatible by Raynaud dominance
(GJR 2.4, and the proof of 7.10).

For an R-PL function g, nefness of its residue at x is equivalent to
existence near x of a nef real formal model of the trivial bundle
(GJR Proposition 7.1). In particular the semipositive locus is ordinary
open (Remark 7.3(4)). Here is the relevant argument. Represent g by a
formal model on a compact strict neighbourhood and restrict to the
closure V of the reduction of x. It is nef by G2. Take a compact strict
neighbourhood inside red^(-1)(V), which is open by anticontinuity;
after an admissible blowup it is the generic fibre of a formal open.
Each proper integral curve in its special fibre maps into V. The
projection formula makes the restricted formal class nef. This also
shows openness at every point of that neighbourhood. Neither a
whole-space property nor an unrelated cover is substituted here.

**G5 (fixed-neighbourhood LFS approximation).** If a GJR R-PL g is
semipositive at an inner point x of a good strict space over nontrivially
valued K, there is one strictly analytic neighbourhood U and a SEQUENCE
of R-PL locally Fubini–Study functions g_j on U converging UNIFORMLY
to g there (GJR Proposition 7.10). LFS means that each residual class is
semiample (Definition 7.8). Locally such functions are nonnegative real
combinations of finite logarithmic maxima of analytic units (7.9).
The complete fixed-neighbourhood mechanism is reconstructed in section 4.

**G6 (the only imported curvature-recognition direction).** On a
boundaryless, topologically separated analytic space, CLD Theorem 18.8.4,
with d=1, says that positivity near x of the first Chern current of an
integral metrized line bundle implies numerical effectiveness of its
residual line bundle. We apply ONLY this implication and ONLY to genuinely
STRICTLY integral metrics. In this strict scope the degree-one residual
bundle is the GJR residue (CLD 10.6.2, 17.5.1, 17.7.10; GJR 6.16), and
numerical effectiveness is precisely nonnegative degree on proper curves
(CLD 10.4.11, 10.7.14; GJR 6.7–6.11). For the trivial bundle of weight b,
the Chern current is dd[b] (CLD 18.1.2). No converse nef-to-Psh statement
from CLD 18.8.8 or 17.8.4 is used.

The positive-to-nef part of the proof of 18.8.4 is independent of the
problematic approximation proof in 17.8.4. It fixes a residual premodel
by a finite logarithmic domain cover. For an integral residual curve C,
the tube above its generic point accumulates at x. Hence it meets any
ordinary neighbourhood on which the Chern current is positive. At a
point of this tube the residual dimension of the metric is at most one;
the dimension-one current formula is a positive analytic multiplicity
times deg_C of the residual bundle. Positivity makes this degree
nonnegative. Repeating for every C is the desired nef implication.
This use preserves source multiplicities and does not classify T.

Standard algebraic inputs used in G3/G5 are stated explicitly: (a) Chow's
lemma as above; (b) for A ample and M a line bundle on a projective variety,
A^N tensor M is ample for all sufficiently large integers N; and (c) a
nef real class plus a positive multiple of an ample class on a projective
variety is ample. These hold over an arbitrary residue field, and are
used only on projective varieties, not arbitrary proper analytic spaces.
For (b), sufficiently high twists of M and M^(-1) by a very ample bundle
are generated by global sections (Serre); the tensor product of a very
ample bundle and a globally generated bundle is very ample, as seen by
the product morphism followed by the Segre embedding. Treat the finitely
many residue classes of the exponent of A to obtain all sufficiently
large N. Input (c) is the projective ample/nef cone theorem, cited in
GJR's actual proof of 7.10 to Lazarsfeld, Positivity I, Corollary 1.4.10.
Its real-Picard version follows by placing the finite coefficient vector
in a small rational cube of ample classes: each rational vertex clears
to an ample integral bundle, and the original vector is their convex
combination. Thus the semiample meaning of GJR 7.8 is respected, including
real, rather than just numerical, representatives.

**C1 (ordinary calculus).** Good separated pure spaces have the canonical
CLD level-n calibrated analytic tropical structure, with analytic boundary
as tropical boundary (CLD Theorem 15.7.1 and 15.7.2). Their boundaryless
ordinary opens are locally differentially separated. Smooth forms and
currents restrict and form ordinary sheaves; compact supports have smooth
cutoffs and finite smooth localization (CLD 6.2, Proposition 7.1.7).
The fundamental integration current is positive and doubly closed by
Stokes, and retains generic nilpotent multiplicities. Logarithms of
analytic units are ordinary CLD-smooth with zero smooth Hessian.

**C2 (middle positivity).** Every symmetric compactly supported ordinary
smooth test is a difference of two compactly supported positive tests
(CLD Proposition 6.5.2; GJR Lemma 7.12). Compact supports in the good
analytic scope satisfy its support hypothesis. Wedges of positive forms
are positive, including a positive smooth (1,1)-form wedged with any
positive complementary test (CLD Proposition 3.5.5, Lemma 7.2.7).
These are middle-positive statements. We supply the multiplication bound
and one-factor argument below, using no assertion that strongly positive
forms span all symmetric forms.

## 3. Direct minimal-sheaf proofs

CPsh is the smallest ORDINARY subsheaf of finite continuous functions
containing smooth functions of positive smooth Hessian and closed under
uniform sequential limits on a fixed open (CLD Definition 8.1.7).

**Lemma 1 (operations).** This definition alone makes CPsh closed under
finite nonnegative real combinations and locally uniform sequential limits.

**Proof.** For fixed c>=0, the sheaf of continuous f such that cf is CPsh
contains the smooth positive-Hessian generators and is uniform-sequentially
closed. Minimality gives scaling (c=0 is immediate). For a fixed smooth
positive-Hessian s on an open O, the sheaf on O of continuous f for which
f+s is CPsh has these same properties. Thus f+s is CPsh whenever f is.
Now on any O consider the sheaf of continuous g with this property:
for every subopen V and every f in CPsh(V), f+g|V is CPsh(V). It is a
sheaf, is closed under uniform sequential limits, and contains every
smooth generator by the preceding argument. Minimality gives addition
of arbitrary CPsh sections. Iteration gives finite nonnegative sums.
All these arguments can be run on an open O: the minimal construction
commutes with restriction, since the generators and the uniform-closure
condition are local sheaf conditions. For locally uniform convergence,
take a compact neighbourhood of a point inside the given open. Convergence
is uniform on its interior; use the definition there and then sheaf
locality. Such compact neighbourhoods exist in our good separated scope.
QED.

No transfinite-stage identity involving Psh was used. The harmless inclusion
CPsh subset Psh, when needed for the worked psh pair in section 7, follows
directly as well: Psh is the minimal sheaf generated by smooth psh functions
and decreasing sequential limits (CLD 8.1.1). It is stable under translation
by constants, by the same minimality argument. If finite Psh functions f_j
converge uniformly to f, then f_j+2||f_j-f|| has a decreasing subsequence
converging to f. Indeed choose successive positive errors with e_next<e/3;
then f_next+2e_next <= f+3e_next <= f_j+e+3e_next <= f_j+2e.
If infinitely many errors are zero, use the constant subsequence instead.
The continuous part of Psh is therefore a uniform-closed sheaf containing
the generators; it contains CPsh. The REVERSE inclusion is not asserted.

**Lemma 2 (logarithmic maxima).** Each real-PL LFS function is locally CPsh.

**Proof.** The local LFS presentation in GJR 7.9 reduces, with Lemma 1,
to L=max(ell_1,...,ell_r), ell_i=+-log|u_i|, possibly with smooth
logarithmic-linear terms and positive denominators. All ell_i are ordinary
smooth and have zero smooth Hessian. On this same ordinary presentation
open define

    S_tau = tau log(sum_i exp(ell_i/tau)),    tau>0.

This is a smooth function of the logarithms of analytic units, hence an
ORDINARY CLD-smooth function. Put w_i=exp(ell_i/tau)/sum_k exp(ell_k/tau).
Its Euclidean Hessian is (diag(w)-w w^t)/tau, and for every real z,

    z^t Hess(S_tau) z
      = (sum_i w_i z_i^2 - (sum_i w_i z_i)^2)/tau
      = sum_{i<j} w_i w_j (z_i-z_j)^2/tau >= 0.

Composing with the common moment of the u_i preserves this positive
(1,1)-form. Equivalently, it is a sum of positive squares of d'(ell_i-ell_j)
with the corresponding d'' terms. There are no scalar Hessian terms from
ell_i. Also

    0 <= S_tau-L <= tau log r

EVERYWHERE on that presentation open, independently of bounds for ell_i.
Taking tau=1/j, Definition 8.1.7 makes L CPsh. Finite positive combinations
and logarithmic-linear terms follow from Lemma 1; ordinary locality gives
the result. This neither asserts harmonicity of S_tau nor uses
Psh intersect C = CPsh. QED.

For completeness, the local expression in 7.9 has a strict proof. Lift the
integral semiample classes in the finite nonnegative real residue
presentation to strict PL germs by G3. An integer power of each residue is
globally generated; strict Corollary 17.5.10 gives the weight of that power
as a finite maximum of logarithms of analytic units after choice of a
normalized frame. The frame normalization is possible in the strict lift:
its fibre has a degree-one basis, which lifts to an analytic frame of norm
1 at x (strict Corollary 17.5.5). Subtracting the lifted real combination
from the original function leaves zero real residue and is real-linear in
ordinary analytic-unit logarithms by GJR Lemma 6.18. Add this smooth
zero-Hessian term to the finite maxima. All neighbourhoods used for this
ONE finite LFS presentation can be intersected once. No approximation
index enters this local expression.

**Lemma 3 (ordinary product bound and one-factor positivity).** For fixed
positive T, locally uniform nets f_a -> f satisfy f_aT -> fT weakly.
For f in CPsh and T positive doubly closed, dd(fT) is positive doubly
closed, in every target degree.

**Proof.** If beta is a fixed symmetric compact test, use C2 to write
beta=beta_1-beta_2 with positive compact tests. Pairing T with a positive
test times a smooth scalar defines a positive scalar distribution; a
smooth cutoff equal to 1 near a compact support bounds this distribution
by its sup norm. Smooth scalar approximation and the Riesz representation
theorem extend it to a positive Radon measure mu_i. This is exactly the
ordinary product construction of CLD 7.2.8 and 7.2.11–12, restricted to
middle-positive T. Thus with C containing both supports,

    |< (f_a-f)T,beta >|
      <= ||f_a-f||_C <T,beta_1+beta_2>.

Symmetry removes the antisymmetric part of any test. This proves the
assertion for the original net. It also justifies the ordinary order-zero
products and all uniform limits used here without relying on the
strongly-positive spanning assertion in the broader wording of 7.2.9.

For the second assertion, on every open consider continuous f for which
dd(fT) is positive. These sets form an ordinary sheaf: positivity and
currents are local, with nonnegative smooth partitions on compact supports.
For smooth f of positive Hessian, the ordinary Leibniz formula and both
closedness equations give dd(fT)=dd f wedge T, positive by C2. Uniform
sequential limits preserve this property by the displayed product bound
applied to beta=dd alpha. Minimality in Definition 8.1.7 includes all CPsh
functions. Distributional identities d'^2=d''^2=0 and d'd''=-d''d' give
double closedness. This is the valid CPsh proof in CLD Lemma 8.5.5;
it needs no Bedford–Taylor premise and no broader Psh recognition. QED.

The same argument with T=delta_X shows that a CPsh function has positive
Hessian current. Both signs of a current being middle-positive imply it
is zero: positive pairings vanish, C2 handles every symmetric test, and
symmetry handles the rest (CLD Remark 7.2.3).

## 4. Fixed-neighbourhood reconstruction of GJR 7.10

**Lemma 4.** A semipositive GJR R-PL germ at an inner point has CPsh
representatives near that point, by uniform LFS approximation on ONE fixed
ordinary open.

**Proof.** Represent g by a real formal class L on a compact strict
neighbourhood Z. The closure of the reduction of x is proper. Choose a
projective proper birational dominating model V, on which a real class M
represents L_g(x), and choose an ample integral line bundle A on V.
By G2, M is nef. Lift A to a strict PL germ b by G3 and represent b by a
formal line bundle B on a compact strict neighbourhood. Use G4 and a
common formal model, shrinking finitely many times, so on a final compact
strict Z both g=h_L and b=h_B, and the reduction closure V' dominates V.
The restrictions of L and B to V' can be made EXACTLY the pullbacks of
M and A. Equality of their residues gives equality after a model refinement;
G4 realizes that refinement as a formal reduction closure. This finite
preparation is done before choosing any approximation parameter.

Let

    O = Int_X(Z) intersect red_Z^(-1)(V').

Anticontinuity makes O ordinary open, and it contains x. For any rational
eta>0 put g_eta=g+eta b. On V its residue representative M+eta A is
ample by the projective nef/ample theorem, hence semiample in the real
meaning of GJR 7.8. Its pullback to V' is semiample. For every y in O,
the closure of red_Z(y) is a closed integral subvariety of V'; restriction
of a semiample real class to it is semiample. That restriction represents
L_(g_eta)(y). Consequently g_eta is LFS at EVERY y of this SAME O.

The function b is continuous on compact Z, hence bounded. With eta=1/j,

    sup_O |g_(1/j)-g| <= ||b||_Z/j -> 0.

Lemma 2 gives CPsh membership for every g_(1/j) on O by ordinary locality.
Definition 8.1.7 now gives g in CPsh(O). The auxiliary compact space may
have a boundary; the current and CPsh conclusion is made only on the
boundaryless ordinary open O. We have intersected with Int_X(Z)
explicitly. No sequence of shrinking O_j is used. QED.

This reconstructs the decisive mechanism of the actual primary
Proposition 7.10, not the incomplete fixed-neighbourhood inference in the
older merely integral metric proof.

## 5. One genuine integral order unit and rational recognition

**Lemma 5 (order unit).** Given finitely many GJR integral PL g_i near x,
there are one bounded ordinary neighbourhood B of x, a bounded GJR
integral PL a on B, and one integer N>=1 such that, as ordinary currents,

    A0=dd[a] >= 0,   N A0 + dd[g_i] >= 0,   N A0 - dd[g_i] >= 0.

**Proof.** Choose a compact strict neighbourhood Y of x inside the
common ordinary domain of all g_i, with x inner. Choose one formal model
dominating the finite models of the g_i. On its proper reduction closure
V_0, their residues are represented by integral line bundles M_i,0.
Use Chow's lemma to get a projective V -> V_0; set M_i to be their
pullbacks. Choose A ample on V and lift its residue to a STRICT integral
PL germ a by G3. After one finite shrinking all functions are defined on
the same ordinary neighbourhood.

By the ample-twist property, choose one N for which A^N tensor M_i and
A^N tensor M_i^(-1) are ample for every i. Thus L_a(x) and
L_(Na+-g_i)(x), represented respectively by A and N A+-M_i, are nef.
By G4 their semipositive loci are open. Intersect the finitely many loci;
Lemma 4 makes a and each Na+-g_i CPsh locally there, hence on that
ordinary intersection. Lemma 3 with delta_X gives the displayed current
inequalities throughout it. Finally choose a compact strict neighbourhood
Y_1 of x inside the intersection and let B=Int_X(Y_1). All functions
extend continuously to Y_1 and are bounded on B. The constant N and
the ordinary open B are fixed once for all. No real constant is appended
to a: it remains genuinely integral strict PL. QED.

**Proof of positive-current recognition.** Write h=sum_i lambda_i g_i on
an ordinary neighbourhood of x using G1, and assume H=dd[h]>=0. Restrict
to the B of Lemma 5. Put B_i=dd[g_i]. Choose epsilon_m=1/m and rational
q_i,m so close that, with r_i,m=q_i,m-lambda_i,

    N sum_i |r_i,m| <= epsilon_m.

Set f_m=sum_i q_i,m g_i+epsilon_m a. The exact current identity is

    dd[f_m] = H + (epsilon_m-N sum_i |r_i,m|) A0
                + sum_(r_i,m!=0) |r_i,m| (N A0+sign(r_i,m) B_i) >= 0.

Every coefficient and every current on the right is nonnegative. This is
an inequality on all positive ordinary tests on the FIXED B; it is not
an inequality after tropical pushforward.

Choose an integer d_m clearing all rational q_i,m and epsilon_m. Then
d_m f_m is strictly integral PL: finite integer sums of strict-domain
logarithms of analytic units have that property on the common strict
G-refinement. No nonstrict covering or arbitrary constant is introduced.
The trivial bundle of norm exp(-d_m f_m) therefore lies in the genuine
strict scope of G6. At every y of B, dd[d_m f_m]>=0 implies its residual
line bundle is nef. Scaling by 1/d_m gives nefness of L_(f_m)(y).

Apply Lemma 4 LOCALLY AT EACH y to f_m. Its LFS approximation takes place
on a fixed neighbourhood of that y; the neighbourhood may depend on m.
This is sufficient: ordinary locality gives f_m in CPsh(B) for every m.
The FINAL limit f_m -> h is on the original, m-INDEPENDENT B, since

    ||f_m-h||_B <= sum_i |r_i,m| ||g_i||_B + epsilon_m ||a||_B -> 0.

Definition 8.1.7 gives h in CPsh(B), then ordinary locality gives
h in CPsh(W). The empty presentation gives zero and is immediate.

To see the residual conclusion independently of any CPsh-to-nef
recognition, use the same projective V of Lemma 5 at x. The class
sum_i q_i,m M_i+epsilon_m A represents L_(f_m)(x); it is nef on V by G2.
For EVERY proper integral curve C in V,

    sum_i q_i,m deg_C(M_i)+epsilon_m deg_C(A) >= 0.

Pass to the limit for each fixed C. The resulting inequality says that
sum_i lambda_i M_i, representing L_h(x), is nef. No compactness of the
set of curves or finite curve test is claimed. Repeat at every x.

Conversely residual nefness gives CPsh by Lemma 4, and CPsh gives
dd[h]>=0 by Lemma 3. This proves statements 1–2 of the main theorem.
QED.

## 6. Zero current, arbitrary T, and nets

If dd[h]=0, apply the preceding recognition to both h and -h. Both are
CPsh locally, hence on W. For arbitrary positive doubly closed T set
R=dd(hT). Lemma 3 makes R and dd((-h)T)=-R middle-positive. The pointedness
argument after that lemma gives R=0. Its test degree is
(n-p-1,n-p-1); for p=n-1 these are scalar tests and for p=0 they are
(n-1,n-1)-tests. All endpoints are included. Locality gives the identity
on W, and UT-VT=(U-V)T gives the target identity for the displayed pair
class. Generic nonreduced multiplicities have not been discarded.

Suppose on one fixed ordinary O a net h_a of GJR R-PL zero-current
functions converges locally uniformly to h. The proved identity holds
for each h_a and any fixed T. For a compactly supported test alpha,
use Lemma 3 on beta=dd alpha (and its symmetric part if needed):

    |<dd((h_a-h)T),alpha>| <= C_(T,alpha) ||h_a-h||_C -> 0.

The compact C and constant are fixed independently of a. Passing to the
limit proves dd(hT)=0. The same estimate for delta_X also gives dd[h]=0,
but does NOT give a real-PL presentation or CPsh membership for h.
Nothing replaces the directed net by a sequence. Applying this locally
and then current locality proves statement 4.

## 7. A concrete nonsmooth analytic surface test

Let K=C((t)) with |t|=e^(-1). Let E be a smooth elliptic curve over C,
with origin o, and choose z in E(C) of infinite order. Such z exists
because E(C) is uncountable while its torsion subgroup is countable.
Let C=(E tensor_C K)^an, with its good-reduction smooth proper model
over C[[t]]. Let x be the divisorial/Gauss point of the special fibre;
the t-adic valuation of that fibre gives this analytic point, and
tilde H(x)=C(E). Its strict germ reduction is represented by the proper
smooth curve E (G2), and Pic(red(C,x))=Pic(E). Indeed the models with
function field C(E) are dominated by the normal proper curve E;
proper birational maps between normal proper curves are isomorphisms.
Thus filtered model Picard groups identify this reduction Picard group
with Pic(E).

The residual line bundle M=O_E(z-o) has degree zero and is non-torsion.
Strict lifting G3 supplies an integral strict PL germ g at x, with
residue M. Both M and M^(-1) are nef on E. G4 gives an ordinary open
B around x on which g and -g are semipositive. Lemma 4 gives both signs
in CPsh(B), hence dd[g]=0 by Lemma 3 and pointedness.

The function g is NOT ordinary CLD-smooth near x. One can use the strict
statement CLD Proposition 17.5.11 (smooth strict metric iff torsion
residue), but the needed direction also has this direct proof. For a
smooth parlinear function, the VALID FIRST PART of CLD Proposition
12.5.4 gives an integer k, an analytic unit u, and a real constant c
with kg=c+log|u| on an ordinary neighbourhood. This follows from a
smooth moment presentation: a smooth rational-PL function is locally
rational-affine, so one clears the slope denominators. Choose a rigid
point y there. Since g is STRICT integral PL and H(y)/K is finite,
g(y) and log|u(y)| are in Q log|K^*|=Q. Consequently c is rational,
and after an integer l, lc=log|b| for b in K^*. Therefore
lk g=log|b u^l| and its residue is zero by GJR Lemma 6.17. This
contradicts non-torsion of M. The argument uses strict values at the
rigid point; it does not accept the broader real-constant normalization
for merely integral functions.

Let D be the standard strict open unit disc and set X=B times_K D,
h=g composed with the projection pi:X->B. This is an ANALYTIC fibre
product, with no identification with a topological cartesian product.
It is good, separated, strict, smooth, boundaryless, and pure of
dimension two. Its section s(b)=(b,0) is analytic.

CPsh pulls back through analytic maps in this precise situation:
smooth positive-Hessian generators pull back as ordinary CLD forms,
uniform sequential limits pull back uniformly, and the condition is
local. Minimality therefore gives pi^*(CPsh_B) subset CPsh_X. Both
h and -h are CPsh. Thus dd[h]=0 and dd(hT)=0 for EVERY positive doubly
closed target current (p=0 or 1). This derives the Hessian equation
from positivity of both pullback functions; it does not pull back an
ordinary current equation. Also h cannot be ordinary CLD-smooth at
s(x), since s^*h=g and ordinary smooth forms pull back analytically.
The pair U=h,V=0 is finite continuous and CLD psh, by the safe inclusion
CPsh subset Psh proved in section 3. It has equal curvature and satisfies
Bedford–Taylor independence on every ordinary subopen of X for this
specified pair. There is no assertion for every other pair on X.

## 8. Source repair, comparison, and residual gap

The old broad class allowed c+log|u| on nonstrict G-domains. This paper
DROPS that class. Clearing rational slopes in it does not make its
metrics strict. For instance max(0,log|z|-c), with c irrational relative
to log|Q_p^*|, keeps an inadmissible strict value-group break after
denominator clearing. The present functions instead start with GJR
Definition 5.1, whose strict integral summands match CLD 12.5.2 exactly.

CLD Corollary 18.8.8's general integral recognition and Proposition
8.1.11's equality Psh intersect C=CPsh are NOT assumed, repaired, or
asserted here. The nef-to-psh citation mismatch and the discontinuous
successor approximants in 8.1.11 remain outside this argument. We have
used only the strict positive-to-nef direction of 18.8.4, direct minimal
CPsh operations, fixed-neighbourhood GJR 7.10, and explicit soft maxima.
The previous rational-cube argument is unnecessary for this repair.
The earlier source-side split audit does not resolve those broader gaps
and is not used to certify the current paper.

The mathematical advance over the prior two-sign criterion is recognition
for the entire ACTUAL GJR real-PL class, including sums with nonzero
individual Hessians that cancel. The order unit and rational corrections
handle positive current as well as zero current. The proof establishes
ordinary CPsh membership, not only classical curve-test psh or weak
smoothness. The source already contains a real-PL-to-residual-harmonic
classification; its reuse is not counted as solving an active atom.

The result retains every target current but restricts the pair difference
and excludes trivial valuation. General continuous differences U-V have
no supplied PL decomposition, residual line bundle, or integral
denominator-clearing step. No theorem makes them locally uniform net
limits of real-PL zero-current functions. Smooth ordinary harmonic
approximation is not obtained: the soft maxima generally have nonzero
positive curvature. The worked surface shows why ordinary nonsmoothness
alone cannot create a defect. A CPsh generation argument for U and V
does not authorize subtracting their approximants while preserving
curvature. No legitimate regularization of an arbitrary h on a fixed
ordinary neighbourhood was constructed, and no admissible counterexample
was found. Universal P21.3 remains unresolved.

## 9. Reproducibility and bibliography

All new files are in this worker workspace; earlier papers, book sources,
Xean and authentication were not edited. Sources are frozen copies in
sources/, with hashes in source-manifest.json. The extracted full review
outputs are review1-extracted.json and review2-extracted.json. The paper
is P21.3-partial.tex; status.json has the sole atomic record.

Rerun: `python audit.py`, then
`pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error P21.3-partial.tex`
twice. The arithmetic checks and compilation check transcription and
reproducibility, not mathematical correctness or independent review.
Environment details are in environment.json; small check outputs are
in logs/. No resource-heavy calculation was needed. No operator-provided
supervised compute service was exposed; none was launched locally.

[CLD] A. Chambert-Loir and A. Ducros, *Formes différentielles réelles et
courants sur les espaces de Berkovich*, arXiv:1204.6277v2, 2025.
https://arxiv.org/abs/1204.6277v2

[GJR] W. Gubler, P. Jell and J. Rabinoff, *Forms on Berkovich spaces based
on harmonic tropicalizations*, arXiv:2111.05741v3, 2025.
https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.05741v3

[Bosch] S. Bosch, *Lectures on Formal and Rigid Geometry*, Lecture Notes
in Mathematics 2105, 2014; Raynaud domination as used and cited in GJR 2.4.

[EGA II] A. Grothendieck, *Éléments de géométrie algébrique II*, Publ.
Math. IHÉS 8 (1961); Chow's lemma and the valuative criterion, in the
exact proper-variety applications of GJR 6.2 and 7.10.

[Lazarsfeld] R. Lazarsfeld, *Positivity in Algebraic Geometry I*, Springer,
2004, Corollary 1.4.10 (projective nef plus ample); the application and
real-class scope are explicitly used in the primary proof of GJR 7.10.

BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: TASK-GRANTED exact fixed-domain compact selection and dual test-measure reduction
# Compact selection and a dual test-measure reduction for P21.3

Research draft, 3 October 2026. The universal P21.3 atom remains unresolved.
The results below are supporting lemmas, not a new Bedford–Taylor positive
range or an analytic counterexample. No priority, formal verification, or
independent-review claim is made.

## 1. Target, scope, and inputs

The target fixes a complete non-Archimedean field \(K\), possibly trivially
valued, a good separated strict boundaryless pure \(n\)-dimensional
\(K\)-analytic space \(X\), \(n\geq2\), an arbitrary **ordinary** open
\(W\subset X\), and a positive, \(d'\)- and \(d''\)-closed ordinary CLD
current \(T\) of bidegree \((p,p)\), \(0\leq p\leq n-1\). Its assertion is

\[
d'd''[U]=d'd''[V]\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
d'd''((U-V)T|_W)=0
\]

for every finite continuous CLD-psh pair \(U,V\) on \(W\). Here
\([f]=f\delta_X\), using the canonical integration current, including
generic component lengths. Positivity means the CLD middle cone.

This draft proves two support results.

1. Transfinite decreasing constructions **on one unchanged domain**, with
   no intermediate sheafification, have a compact upper-selection property.
   Their finite continuous outputs are locally decreasing uniform limits
   of smooth psh functions on a neighbourhood fixed before the sequence.
   Discontinuous intermediate functions cause no problem in this result.
2. For a fixed target current, annihilation of **all continuous
   zero-current functions** is equivalent to a precise weak closure
   condition on signed test measures. This is an exact dual reformulation
   of a stronger multiplier property, not a proof that the condition holds.

Neither result assumes nontrivial valuation. Neither recognizes arbitrary
continuous positive-Hessian functions as CPsh, constructs curvature-matched
approximants for a pair, or supplies a density theorem for currents.

The accepted strict real-PL theorem and its full proof and audit were read
in the supplied deliverables. Their scope is complete **nontrivially**
valued fields, actual GJR real-PL differences and their stated
fixed-neighbourhood locally uniform zero-current net closure. That theorem
is not a premise of either proof here. Its review does not certify these
new results or a changed recognition theorem. The prior elliptic-disc
surface, coarse fan, scalar-ascent, and strong-product attempts were read
and are not counted as new counterexamples.

The primary source is [CLD], arXiv:1204.6277v2, 25 July 2025. The exact
inputs needed here are:

* Definition 8.1.1: Psh is the smallest ordinary subsheaf of functions
  with values in \(\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}\) containing smooth functions
  with positive smooth Hessian and stable under decreasing sequential
  limits. The printed definition itself has no local-integrability
  clause; Lemma 8.1.12 separately treats locally integrable sections.
  The selection argument also works if local integrability is required
  at every limit, since it only uses an existing construction.
* Definition 8.1.7: CPsh is the smallest ordinary subsheaf of finite
  continuous functions containing the same smooth generators and stable
  under uniform sequential limits. Lemmas 8.1.4 and 8.1.8 describe the
  respective **sheaf** stages: local decreasing/uniform limits at
  successors and local earlier-stage membership at limits.
* Section 11.1.7: an analytic space has a basis of compact neighbourhoods;
  good spaces have affinoid neighbourhood bases. In the separated scope
  the underlying space is Hausdorff. Thus a point of an ordinary open
  has a compact neighbourhood contained in that open, whose ordinary
  interior still contains the point.
* Theorem 15.7.1 and Section 15.7.2: a topologically separated pure
  analytic space has the canonical calibrated tropical structure of its
  dimension, and restriction to an analytic domain agrees with its own
  structure. Boundaryless ordinary opens are locally differentially
  separated. No valuation or reducedness restriction is imposed.
* Proposition 6.5.2: a symmetric compactly supported smooth \((q,q)\)
  test is a difference of two middle-positive compactly supported smooth
  tests, provided its support has the stated paracompact and locally
  differentially separated neighbourhood. Its analytic support
  hypothesis holds for compact supports on an almost-good space, hence
  in the good boundaryless scope here.
* Sections 7.2.8, 7.2.11–12: a positive current paired with a complementary
  positive smooth test gives a positive Radon coefficient measure;
  locally bounded Borel functions have the ordinary order-zero product.
  We give the needed signed-measure construction below using middle
  positivity and the preceding positive-test decomposition. We do not
  use a claim that strongly positive tests span all symmetric tests.
* The ordinary current differential is dual to the test differential;
  for symmetric equal-bidegree tests the convention gives
  \(\langle d'd''(hT),\alpha\rangle
  =\langle hT,d'd''\alpha\rangle\). This is the convention used in
  Lemma 7.7.5 and Section 8.5.3. The calculus has
  \(d'^2=d''^2=0\), \(d'd''=-d''d'\), and \(Jd'd''=d'd''J\).

The compact-selection proof below is elementary and proves its own Dini
statement. The dual-closure proof uses a finite-dimensional separation
argument given in full. Proposition 8.1.11's disputed reverse inclusion
and its same-rank assertion are not imported.

## 2. Compact upper selection for upper semicontinuous sequences

**Lemma 1.** Let \(C\) be a compact topological space. Let
\(v_j:C\to\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}\) be upper semicontinuous and
decrease pointwise to \(v\). Let \(F:C\to\mathbb R\) be continuous and
assume \(v<F\) everywhere. Then some \(j\) satisfies \(v_j<F\)
everywhere on \(C\).

**Proof.** Each \(E_j=\{v_j<F\}\) is open in \(C\). They increase
with \(j\) and their union is \(C\), because of the strict pointwise
inequality and convergence. A finite subcover exists. Taking the largest
of its finitely many indices gives a single \(E_j=C\). This proof needs
neither metrizability nor continuity of the \(v_j\). \(\square\)

**Corollary 1 (upper semicontinuous Dini).** If the same sequence has a
finite continuous limit \(u\), it converges uniformly on \(C\).

**Proof.** Decrease gives \(v_j\geq u\). Apply Lemma 1 to
\(F=u+\varepsilon\) for each \(\varepsilon>0\). Eventually
\(0\leq v_j-u<\varepsilon\) on \(C\). This is precisely uniform
convergence. \(\square\)

Thus discontinuity of decreasing successor approximants does not itself
invalidate the Dini conclusion for a continuous finite output. However,
being a uniform approximant is not being a **CPsh** uniform approximant.
That distinction matters in the full sheaf construction.

## 3. A construction with one fixed domain

Fix a locally compact Hausdorff space \(O\) and a family
\(\mathcal S(O)\subset C(O,\mathbb R)\) of generators. Define classes
of functions with values in \(\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}\) by

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal M_0(O)&=\mathcal S(O),\\
\mathcal M_{\alpha+1}(O)&=\mathcal M_\alpha(O)\cup
 \{\inf_j v_j: v_j\in\mathcal M_\alpha(O),\ v_{j+1}\leq v_j\},\\
\mathcal M_\lambda(O)&=\bigcup_{\beta<\lambda}\mathcal M_\beta(O)
 \quad (\lambda\text{ a nonzero limit ordinal}).
\end{aligned}
\]

Write \(\mathcal M(O)\) for their eventual union. This is a construction
in the set of functions on the fixed set \(O\); equivalently it is the
smallest class containing the generators and stable under decreasing
sequences. One may use the stages up to their stabilization: a strictly
increasing chain cannot add a new function at every stage beyond the
cardinality of this function set. In particular every member has an
ordinal rank. There is **no** gluing operation at successor stages and
**no** local earlier-rank membership at limit stages.

For the analytic generators, each \(\mathcal M_\alpha(O)\) is contained
in Psh\((O)\): the claim holds at rank zero, the Psh closure axiom
handles successors, and unions handle limit ranks. This inclusion does
not identify the fixed-domain class with the generated sheaf.

Every member is upper semicontinuous. This follows by transfinite induction:
the generators are continuous, a decreasing limit is upper
semicontinuous since \(\{\inf_jv_j<a\}=\bigcup_j\{v_j<a\}\), and
limit stages take unions of classes, not a new limit of functions.

**Theorem 1 (compact selection at arbitrary fixed-domain rank).** For
every \(v\in\mathcal M(O)\), every compact \(C\subset O\), and every
continuous \(F:C\to\mathbb R\) with \(v|_C<F\), there is a generator
\(s\in\mathcal S(O)\) such that

\[
v\leq s\text{ on all of }O,\qquad s|_C<F.
\]

**Proof.** Induct on the stage containing \(v\). At rank zero use
\(s=v\). At a successor, a member already in the previous class is
covered by induction. Otherwise it is the decreasing limit of
\(v_j\in\mathcal M_\alpha(O)\). Lemma 1 selects an index \(j\) with
\(v_j<F\) on the **same** compact \(C\). The induction hypothesis
selects \(s\in\mathcal S(O)\) satisfying \(v_j\leq s\) on all of
\(O\) and \(s<F\) on \(C\). Since \(v\leq v_j\) everywhere,
this \(s\) works. At a limit stage, \(v\) belongs on all of \(O\) to
one earlier class; apply that case. The induction never shrinks \(O\)
or \(C\). This proves the statement for every rank. \(\square\)

Notice that the limit at an intermediate node need not be continuous. The
continuous function is the strict upper obstacle \(F\) on the compact.
The selection of a single child does not assert uniform convergence to a
discontinuous intermediate limit.

**Theorem 2 (continuous output collapses to the first local stage).**
Let \(O\) be an ordinary open of the target analytic space. Take
\(\mathcal S(O)\) to be the ordinary CLD-smooth functions with positive
smooth Hessian on \(O\). Suppose \(u\in\mathcal M(O)\) is finite and
continuous. For every point \(x\in O\), choose **before** the
approximation index a compact neighbourhood \(C\subset O\), and put
\(B=\operatorname{Int}_O(C)\), with \(x\in B\). There is a sequence
of smooth psh functions on this one \(B\) which decreases to \(u|_B\)
and converges uniformly there. In particular \(u\in\mathrm{CPsh}(O)\)
and locally belongs to both first stages Psh\(_1\) and CPsh\(_1\).

**Proof.** Set \(\varepsilon_m=3^{-m}\). Theorem 1 with
\(v=u\) and \(F=u|_C+\varepsilon_m\) gives a smooth psh
\(s_m\) defined on **all of the original** \(O\) with

\[
u\leq s_m\text{ on }O,\qquad
0\leq s_m-u<\varepsilon_m\text{ on }C.
\]

Define \(q_m=s_m+\varepsilon_m\), and restrict to \(B\). Addition
of a constant does not change its smooth Hessian. On \(B\),

\[
u+\varepsilon_m\leq q_m<u+2\varepsilon_m,
\qquad
q_{m+1}<u+2\varepsilon_{m+1}
 <u+\varepsilon_m\leq q_m.
\]

Thus \(q_m\) decreases on this fixed \(B\) and
\(\sup_B|q_m-u|\leq2\varepsilon_m\to0\). The first-stage
conclusions follow from the definitions; minimal CPsh and its ordinary
sheaf property give the result on \(O\). No positivity was obtained by
a partition of unity. No sequence of approximation-dependent domains or
intersection of infinitely many neighbourhoods occurs. \(\square\)

For precision one can sheafify **once, after** the fixed-domain
construction: let \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{loc}}(W)\) consist of functions
which locally belong to \(\mathcal M(O)\) on an ordinary open
\(O\subset W\). Restriction of each rank construction commutes with
restriction of the domain, by transfinite induction. Therefore this local
membership rule is a sheaf.

**Corollary 2.** As ordinary sheaves of finite continuous functions,

\[
C\cap\mathcal M_{\mathrm{loc}}
 =\mathrm{CPsh}_1=C\cap\mathrm{Psh}_1.
\]

**Proof.** Theorem 2 gives the first inclusion into both first stages.
For the reverse inclusion from CPsh\(_1\), work on a presentation open
\(N\) where smooth psh functions converge uniformly to \(u\). Choose
members \(s_m\) with
\(\sup_N|s_m-u|<\delta_m\), \(\delta_m=4^{-m}\), and set
\(r_m=s_m+2\delta_m\). Then

\[
u+\delta_m<r_m<u+3\delta_m,
\qquad r_{m+1}<u+3\delta_{m+1}<u+\delta_m<r_m.
\]

They decrease to \(u\) on \(N\). Thus
\(u\in\mathcal M_1(N)\) and \(u\in\mathrm{Psh}_1(N)\).
Conversely a continuous Psh\(_1\) presentation is a decreasing sequence
of smooth functions on one open; Corollary 1, or Theorem 2, gives a
uniform sequence on a fixed compact-neighbourhood interior. Ordinary
locality yields the equality claimed. \(\square\)

This does **not** identify \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{loc}}\) with the full
Psh sheaf. In the full Psh construction a preceding-rank section can have
only local presentations, on different opens. A compact \(C\) selected
at the root may not lie in any one of them. Theorem 1 then cannot descend
to a single child defined on all of that \(C\). A finite cover gives
several local generators, not one positive global generator. We have
proved no positive gluing theorem for those generators. Nor have we shown
that the once-sheafified class is stable under all decreasing sequences.
No same-rank version of CLD 8.1.11 is asserted.

## 4. Signed test measures and the stronger continuous kernel property

We work now on an arbitrary ordinary \(W\) in the target, with the fixed
current \(T\). Let \(C(W)\) be all finite real continuous functions,
including those not globally bounded. Let \(\mathscr M_c(W)\) be the
real vector space of finite signed Radon measures with compact support.
Each such measure integrates every member of \(C(W)\). Equip this vector
space with the weak topology \(\sigma(\mathscr M_c(W),C(W))\): its
neighbourhoods are prescribed by finitely many continuous function
pairings. This is not convergence only against smooth functions.

If \(\beta\) is a symmetric compactly supported smooth
\((n-1,n-1)\)-form, define a signed measure \(\nu_\beta\) by

\[
\int_W f\,d\nu_\beta
 =\langle f\delta_X,d'd''\beta\rangle
 =\langle d'd''[f],\beta\rangle,\qquad f\in C(W).
\]

If \(\alpha\) is a symmetric compactly supported smooth
\((n-p-1,n-p-1)\)-form, similarly define \(\nu_{T,\alpha}\) by

\[
\int_W f\,d\nu_{T,\alpha}
 =\langle fT,d'd''\alpha\rangle
 =\langle d'd''(fT),\alpha\rangle.
\]

Here and below all current products are the ordinary products, and all
integration multiplicities belong to the original analytic calibration.

**Lemma 2 (existence of the signed test measures).** These formulas
define measures in \(\mathscr M_c(W)\), supported respectively in
\(\operatorname{supp}\beta\) and \(\operatorname{supp}\alpha\).

**Proof.** Let \(\eta\) stand for the complementary test
\(d'd''\alpha\); it is symmetric. Proposition 6.5.2 gives
\(\eta=\eta_1-\eta_2\), where \(\eta_i\) are positive compact
tests. Middle positivity of \(T\) makes
\(g\mapsto\langle T,g\eta_i\rangle\) a positive scalar
distribution. A smooth cutoff equal to one on the relevant compact gives
its sup-norm bound. Smooth scalar density (CLD 6.2.5–6) extends it
uniquely to a positive Radon measure, which is the coefficient measure of
CLD 7.2.11. Its mass is \(\langle T,\eta_i\rangle\).

Their difference is a finite signed compact measure; pairing it with any
continuous \(f\) is the ordinary product of CLD 7.2.12. This definition
is independent of the decomposition: the pairings agree for all smooth
scalars, and smooth scalar density makes the Radon measures identical.
Outside \(\operatorname{supp}\eta\), smooth scalar pairings vanish;
the same density there proves that the signed measure has zero
restriction. Therefore its support is contained in
\(\operatorname{supp}\eta\subset\operatorname{supp}\alpha\).
The case \(T=\delta_X\) is the same argument. The source support
hypotheses hold on the good boundaryless ordinary open, as stated in
Section 1. No spanning assertion for strongly positive forms is used.
\(\square\)

Write

\[
\mathscr L_W=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{\nu_\beta:\beta
 \text{ as above}\},\qquad
\mathscr H_W=\{h\in C(W):d'd''[h]=0\}.
\]

By symmetry of the function Hessian and the current convention,
\(\mathscr H_W\) is exactly the annihilator of \(\mathscr L_W\) in
\(C(W)\). Testing symmetric forms suffices; antisymmetric forms pair to
zero.

In fact the ambient test space is linear and
\(\nu_{\beta_1}+a\nu_{\beta_2}=\nu_{\beta_1+a\beta_2}\), so its
image already equals the displayed real span. The span notation emphasizes
that no positivity restriction is imposed on these approximating measures.

**Theorem 3 (dual reduction).** For the fixed \(T\), the following
are equivalent:

1. For every \(h\in\mathscr H_W\),
   \(d'd''(hT)=0\).
2. For every test \(\alpha\) of the displayed degree,

   \[
   \nu_{T,\alpha}\in
   \overline{\mathscr L_W}^{\,\sigma(\mathscr M_c(W),C(W))}.
   \]

**Proof.** If condition 2 holds, the continuous evaluation
\(\nu\mapsto\int h\,d\nu\) vanishes on \(\mathscr L_W\) for
\(h\in\mathscr H_W\), hence on its weak closure. It therefore
vanishes on every \(\nu_{T,\alpha}\), which is condition 1.

Conversely suppose a particular \(\nu=\nu_{T,\alpha}\) is outside
that closure. By the definition of the weak topology there are finitely
many \(f_1,\ldots,f_m\in C(W)\) and a positive tolerance for which
the corresponding basic neighbourhood of \(\nu\) misses
\(\mathscr L_W\). Define

\[
P:\mathscr M_c(W)\longrightarrow\mathbb R^m,
\qquad P(\mu)=(\int f_i\,d\mu)_{i=1}^m.
\]

Then \(P(\nu)\notin P(\mathscr L_W)\), for equality would produce
a measure of \(\mathscr L_W\) inside the forbidden neighbourhood.
The image \(P(\mathscr L_W)\) is a vector subspace of the
finite-dimensional space \(\mathbb R^m\), hence closed. There is a
vector \(c\) orthogonal to that subspace with
\(c\cdot P(\nu)\ne0\): take the nonzero orthogonal projection of
\(P(\nu)\) onto the complement. Put \(h=\sum_i c_i f_i\).
It is finite continuous, annihilates every member of
\(\mathscr L_W\), and does not annihilate \(\nu\). Thus
\(d'd''[h]=0\) but
\(\langle d'd''(hT),\alpha\rangle\ne0\), contradicting
condition 1. This proves the converse without importing a separation or
current-density theorem. \(\square\)

The closure may require a **net** of finite real linear combinations of
ambient Hessian test measures. It is not a positive-current approximation
and need not have common compact support or a uniform total-variation
bound. The convergence topology tests all of \(C(W)\); convergence only
against smooth scalars would not prove the stated implication for a
continuous \(h\). The theorem does not assert that any of these nets
exists for arbitrary \(T\).

Condition 1 is stronger than the target's property: the target uses only
\(h=U-V\) with finite continuous psh \(U,V\). Failure of condition 2
would supply a continuous analytic zero-current multiplier defect, but
the finite-dimensional separation proof does **not** make its \(h\)
a difference of finite continuous psh functions. Such failure alone would
therefore still not be an admissible P21.3 counterexample. No failure of
condition 2 is established in this draft.

## 5. What is still missing for a pair

The new selection argument concerns individual functions. Even if a full
Psh-to-CPsh conversion were later repaired, for \(h=U-V\) it would give
\(U,V\in\mathrm{CPsh}\), not
\(h,-h\in\mathrm{CPsh}\). CPsh is a cone. No operation in its
definition subtracts two positive approximations while preserving a
curvature identity. The equality \(d'd''[U]=d'd''[V]\) is upstairs
against \(\delta_X\); it supplies no equality of the finitely indexed
approximants or of their products with an arbitrary singular \(T\).

For a fixed \(T\) and fixed test \(\alpha\), Lemma 2 also gives the
legitimate estimate

\[
|\langle d'd''((f-g)T),\alpha\rangle|
 \leq \|f-g\|_{C^0(C)}\,
 \langle T,\eta_1+\eta_2\rangle,
\]

where \(C\) contains the positive-test decomposition supports for
\(d'd''\alpha=\eta_1-\eta_2\). The compact and coefficient are
fixed before the function approximation index. Consequently uniform
scalar approximation **does** control these differentiated pairings
against a fixed test. What it does not do is identify the limit as zero.
If \(h_j\to h\) locally uniformly, it shows
\(d'd''(h_jT)\to d'd''(hT)\). An additional argument is needed to
show that this limit vanishes. Distributional
\(d'd''[h_j]\to0\) against the ambient integration current supplies
no comparison with the signed measures \(\nu_{T,\alpha}\).

This distinction prevents a false diagnostic: when \(h_j\to0\)
locally uniformly, the displayed estimate already forces
\(d'd''(h_jT)\to0\). There cannot be a nonzero fixed-test defect in
that situation. The missing case has a potentially nonzero continuous
zero-current limit \(h\), not a zero scalar limit.

One possible independent route is now expressed exactly by Theorem 3:
prove its dual closure for the actual analytic test measures. No coarse
tropical pushforward, scalar extension of an arbitrary current, strong
current product, or weak-current density is a premise for that route.

## 6. Self-audit and comparison with the residual atom

Theorem 1 uses a continuous strict upper obstacle on one fixed compact
and preserves the lower inequality on the entire domain. It does not
use false uniform convergence to a discontinuous intermediate limit.
At limit ranks its earlier-rank membership is global by definition;
substituting local membership would invalidate that proof step.
Theorem 2 chooses its compact neighbourhood before \(m\), has smooth
positive Hessian at every finite step, and explicitly makes a decreasing
sequence. The one-time sheafification in Corollary 2 is distinguished
from sheafification between ordinal stages. These qualifications are
mathematical hypotheses, not cosmetic language.

Theorem 3 keeps the current arbitrary and treats every target degree,
including scalar tests at \(p=n-1\). Positive-test decomposition and
ordinary coefficient measures retain calibrations and generic lengths.
Its weak topology is spelled out, its necessity proof constructs a
continuous witness only, and no psh difference is inferred from that
witness. Neither theorem uses an unproved external recognition result.
They apply over trivial as well as nontrivial valuations, but provide no
new universal trivial-valuation Bedford–Taylor result.

The full Psh sheaf conversion still lacks a positive gluing argument on a
neighbourhood fixed before the tolerance. The universal kernel step
still lacks either two-sign recognition, a curvature-compatible
fixed-domain approximation/estimate, the dual closure of Theorem 3, or
another direct multiplier argument. No analytic counterexample satisfying
the equal-curvature and psh-pair premises was found. The already accepted
real-PL result is neither repeated as the new theorem nor counted as a
solution of the residual atom. The status is **partial supporting
progress; P21.3 unresolved**.

## 7. Reproducibility and bibliography

All new outputs are in this worker directory. The standalone paper
`P21.3-partial.tex` contains the full proof bodies. Frozen inputs and
their SHA256 hashes are in `sources/` and `source-manifest.json`;
`sources/checked-passages.txt` contains the actual CLD source extracts.
`research-notes.md` records the failed universal routes and concrete gaps.
`status.json` is the sole target status. `environment.json` records the
execution environment. `self-audit.json` separates proof obligations
from arithmetic diagnostics.

Rerun `bash rerun.sh`. It checks frozen input hashes, runs the small exact
diagnostics in `diagnostics.py`, rebuilds TeX from this argument using
`build-paper.py`, and compiles twice using the already installed TeX.
The diagnostics and compiler output do not establish mathematical
correctness. No resource-heavy calculation, installation, network call,
subagent, or modification of earlier campaigns was used. No
operator-provided supervised compute service was exposed.

[CLD] A. Chambert-Loir and A. Ducros, *Formes différentielles réelles et
courants sur les espaces de Berkovich*, arXiv:1204.6277v2, 2025.
<https://arxiv.org/abs/1204.6277v2>.

[GJR, contextual input only] W. Gubler, P. Jell and J. Rabinoff, *Forms on
Berkovich spaces based on harmonic tropicalizations*, arXiv:2111.05741v3,
2025. <https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.05741v3>. The accepted theorem's GJR
real-PL scope is read as supplied context, not used to prove these lemmas.

---
title: Ordinary zero-Hessian recognition on strict analytic curves over arbitrary fields
author: P06.2 integration research draft
date: 4 October 2026
geometry: margin=1in
fontsize: 11pt
---

## Result and conventions

This document gives a complete integration argument in the curve scope using
the supplied exact supporting theorems n700 and n701 and the stated primary
foundations. The new integration is an unverified draft, with an author audit.
The independent receipts for the supporting theorems are preserved at their
own scopes; no earlier verdict certifies this integration. Original P06.2
remains open in arbitrary dimension, so its status is **partial** and
`candidate=false`. P05.2 is not addressed.

All spaces are actual Berkovich analytic spaces, with their specified
structures. A curve means a separated analytic space pure of analytic
dimension one, as in Gubler--Rabinoff (GR) 3.1. An ordinary open is open in
the underlying topology. Strictness always means strict analyticity over
the current ground field. Put $D=d'd''$. All tests and currents in the
initial equation are **ordinary** Chambert--Loir--Ducros (CLD) tests and
currents. On a curve,
\[
 D[h](\psi)=\int_X h(D\psi)_{\mathrm{Rad},K},
 \qquad \psi\in A_c^{0,0}(X).                         \tag{1}
\]
Canonical integration retains all actual generic Artinian lengths of $X$,
including unequal lengths on different components and nilpotent structure.
The smooth top form $D\psi$ has a signed Radon measure. We do not assume
that $D[h]$ itself is a Radon measure.

GR-subharmonicity means GR Definition 3.4; GR-harmonicity means both signs
are subharmonic (Definition 3.13). Classical pluriharmonicity means both
signs are classically psh in Definition 4.1 (Definition 7.1). These
definitions include extension fields and curve maps. Real PL below has
the **G-local** convention of GR 2.16 and GJR Definition 5.1, on the
G-topology generated by strict analytic domains. GJR-harmonicity means
Definition 7.2, equivalently the numerical triviality criterion in
Remark 7.3(1). It is a different regularity assertion from ordinary CLD
smooth harmonicity.

**Theorem (arbitrary-field intrinsic curve recognition).** Let $K$ be any
complete nontrivially valued non-Archimedean field. Let $X$ be a good,
separated, strict, boundaryless, pure one-dimensional $K$-analytic space.
Every finite continuous $h:X\to\mathbb R$ satisfying (1) equal to zero
for every ordinary compact smooth scalar test is GR-harmonic and
classically pluriharmonic. It is real PL in the stated G-local convention,
and GJR-harmonic. The result holds on every ordinary open subspace of $X$.

More explicitly, for **every** analytic extension field $L/K$, **every**
$L$-analytic curve $Y$, and **every** $L$-analytic morphism
$f:Y\to X_L$, the finite continuous function
\[
                         h\circ\pi_{L/K}\circ f           \tag{2}
\]
is GR-harmonic. No smoothness, strictness, boundarylessness, reducedness,
or separability hypothesis is added to these test curves or maps beyond
being an analytic curve and morphism in the published definition. If
$Y$ is smooth and strict, (2) is also Thuillier-harmonic. The ground-field
theorem includes imperfect and positive-characteristic fields,
singular curves, arbitrary nilpotents, and unequal generic lengths.
There is no global algebraic-model or properness hypothesis.

complete frozen proof bodies were supplied in the input and are copied
unchanged into this workspace. Historical note labels inside those bodies
refer to the original campaigns. They are not new native support IDs.
